<!-- page 1 -->
NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS’ MINUTES 
Tuesday, October 11, 2016 
 
PRESENT:  Dawnafe Whitesinger, Supervisor; Robert K. Black, Jr., Supervisor; Steve Williams, Supervisor; 
Jesse Thompson, Vice-Chairman; and Jason E. Whiting, Chairman, were present in person  
9:02 a.m.  Call To Order: Pledge of Allegiance and Invocation was led by Chairman Whiting.  
CALL TO THE PUBLIC: There were no comments from the public.  
CONSENT AGENDA:  a) Minutes: September 27, 2016 b) Warrant and Voucher Report over $1000 September 
2016 c) Constable Reports: Show Low Precinct #5, September 2016  d) Constable Ethics, Standards & Training 
Board Grant Award Contract #CNA17-202 for $3,777.13 for ballistic vests and equipment for new constables e) 
Reissuance of Stale Dated Check #483464 in the amount of $250.00 f) Department of Public Safety Memorandum 
of Understanding 2016-134 between the Sheriff’s Office and State of Arizona, Department of Public Safety for 
$70,000 to be used pursuant to A.R.S. §41 -1724 g) Contract 2016-AL-028 with Governor’s Office of Highway 
Safety for $13,700 for overtime and capital to support and enhance the Impaired Driver/DUI enforcement program 
h) Contracts signed by the County Manager pursuant to Board of Supervisors Resolution: Woodruff Pit License 
i) Certify revenue collected by the Superior Court, Clerk of the Court and all six Justice  Courts j) Approval of 1 
Back Tax Land Parcel sold over the counter in the amount of $546.44: APN 105-61-056:  Supervisor Thompson 
made a motion to authorize the Chairman to sign the items in the consent agenda; motion seconded by  
Supervisor Whitesinger. Vote unanimous approving the motion.  
BOARD OF SUPERVISORS:  Presentation by Navajo County Fair Incorporated of the 2015 Navajo County 
Fair ceremonial crowning of the Navajo County Rodeo Queens : Michael Sample advised the Board that he 
believes the 2016 fai r was the best fair that we have had in a long time.  He thanked the Board, Department 
Directors and Elected officials for supporting the fair and allowing their employees to volunteer during the fair. 
He spoke about the Queen Pageant.  Queen Director, Senette Buntin, addressed the Board regarding the four 
young ladies who were selected who will be representing Navajo County Fair and Navajo County throughout the 
State of Arizona through fundraisers and will promote the sport of rodeo.  She stated that this year, the outgoing 
queen will be given a $500 scholarship to further her education. Elizabeth Siegfried, 2017 Miss Navajo County 
Fair and Rodeo Queen introduced herself and her mother and her little sister. Lucy Vaughan, 2017 Navajo County 
Fair and Rodeo Teen Queen, introduced herself and her mother. Brooke Suter, 2017 Navajo County Fair and 
Rodeo Princess, introduced herself, her mom and her grandma. She indicated that she is a barrel racer.  R.E. 
Dutcher, Navajo County Fair and Rodeo Lil Buckaroo, introduced herself, her two grandmas and her Grandpa 
Mike, who taught her everything she knows. The Board presented the young ladies with their crowns and thanked 
them for being present today and also thanked their families for what they do for these young ladies.  
PUSH PROGRAM:  Presentation to September and October Wellness Warriors of the Month, Kya Carroll  & 
Erik Jackson: Callie Jensen presented the September and October Wellness Warriors of the Month. She spoke 
about Kya Carroll and what has motivated her and how the PUSH program has helped her. Ms. Jensen introduced 
Erik Jackson and spoke about his motivation to live a healthier lifestyle. Supervisor Williams congratulated Mr. 
Jackson. Ms. Jensen further spoke about the 2nd annual Color Me Strong 5k on October 22nd, 2016, at 8:00 a.m.,

<!-- page 2 -->
with registration starting at 7:00 a.m., here at the Navajo County walking trail. She asked for volunteers to throw 
paint and pass out water.  Supervisor Thompson stated that he supports the PUSH Program.  
SUPERIOR COURT: Updates within the judiciary: Honorable Michala Ruechel updated the Board regarding 
the judiciary. She stated that Superior Court will have approximately 2600 new cases this year if they stay on the 
current track and approximately 25,000 cases filed in Jus tice Court for this year.  She stated that the Courts, 
through the Probation Department, will continue to monitor hundreds of adults and juveniles, and the Clerk of 
the Court will process about 2500 motions or 600,000 to 900,000 pieces of paper.  She state d that the Courts 
typically add $1.5 million to $1.6 million to the general fund from fines and fees.  She indicated that they are 
working to fully staff the Juvenile Detention Facility as budgeted, continue to improve security for County 
buildings, and make sure that adult and juvenile probations are fully staffed.  She stated that during her tenure, 
without an increase in general fund salaries or staff, but with an increased dedication and hard work by employees 
and volunteers and with support of Arizona Supreme Court, various grants, and assistance from the County, the 
Superior Court has handled more than 25,000 cases, has supervised thousands of individuals, handled hundreds 
of thousands of motions, and the Clerk of the Court has processed over four mill ion pieces of paper, and the 
Justice Courts have handled over 200,000 pieces of paper.  She spoke about the Volunteer Lawyers Program.  She 
spoke about encouraging employees to participate in the National Center for State Courts Fellowship Program 
and the County Leadership Programs.  She also spoke about initiating Dependency Team Meetings and a training 
requirement for local attorneys who act as Guardians ad Litem.  She spoke about successfully collaborating, 
establishing and/or maintaining Restitution Cou rt, Veteran’ s Court, and Drug Court.  She spoke about the 
Language Access Plan .  She spoke about working on timeliness and efficiency of case processing, pre -trial 
expansion to utilize evidence-based practices to improve process of identifying release conditions for individuals 
pending trial.  She spoke about security at the County complex and working to enhance the security of our 
facilities.  She spoke about how the Court has helped the public through the Court website.  She further spoke 
about all of the  technical upgrades to the Courts.  She spoke about revamping the way the Courts do indigent 
defense contracts with private attorneys and expressed appreciation for those attorneys who are willing to take 
these cases.  She stated that Dave Byers, Court Administrator for the Supreme Court, asked her to serve a fourth 
term as Presiding Judge. She spoke about her good fortune of working in Navajo County  and all of the support 
she has received from Court Administration, Clerk of the Court, Probation, and especially her Judicial Assistant, 
Glenda Walters.  She spoke about the County Attorney’s Office, Public Defender’s Office, and Legal Defender’s 
Office and how they are committed to providing the best legal representation and committed to the ideals of 
fairness, justice, and security for our citizens.  She thanked her family for their support.  She indicated that she 
believes that it is in the best interests of an organization to have change once in a while, and, therefore, she is not 
going to serve a fourth term as Presiding Judge . She stated that the Honorable Robert J. Higgins has been 
appointed as the Presiding Judge of Navajo County Superior Court, beginning January 1, 2017. Judge Ru echel 
spoke about Judge Higgins and indicated that he will do an outstanding job. Honorable Robert J. Higgins 
addressed the Board thanking Judge Ruechel, Marla Randall and Judge Dale Nielson. He indicated that he looks 
forward to working with the Board and spoke about his goal of having a courtroom in the south County area. 
Supervisor Williams commented about Judge Ruechel’s admirable work. He indicated that Judge Ruechel has a 
servant’s heart and is always doing what is best for the judicial system and people. He thanked Judge Ruechel for 
the example she has set for so many people.  Supervisor Whitesinger thanked Judge Ruechel for her work in 
Navajo County. Supervisor Thompson spoke about Judge Ruechel’s tenure. County Manager, James Jayne, 
thanked Judge Ruechel for finding solutions and partnering with Administration during these difficult financial 
times. Supervisor Black  thanked Judge Ruechel for having be en a part of our family. Chairman Whiting 
thanked Judge Ruechel for continuing to provide services while being fiscally responsible.

<!-- page 3 -->
SHERIFF: Presentation of “Prescription Drug A buse in Tribal Communities” : Sheriff Clark spoke about 
prescription drug abuse in Navajo County and in the Tribal Communities. He spoke of a tool kit that has just 
come out to help combat prescription drug abuse, indicating that 200 tool kits have been handed out to at least 40 
tribes across the County. He showed a Power Point presentation that is contained in the tool kit. Sheriff Clark 
spoke about the prescription take back, which is a national initiative going on October 22 from 10:00 a.m. to 1:00 
p.m. at the Safeway in both Holbrook and Winslow. Supervisor Thompson and Supervisor Whitesinger both 
thanked Sheriff Clark for bringing these initiatives to the Board. Supervisor Whiting thanked Sheriff Clark for 
helping find solutions to these hard problems.  
ADMINISTRATIVE SERVICES:  
a. Consideration and possible approval of Personnel Actions: Katherine Wright reviewed the 23-item personnel 
action report. Supervisor Thompson made a motion to approve the Personnel Action Report as presented; 
motion seconded by Supervisor Williams. Vote unanimous approving the motion.  
b. Consideration and possible approval of a proclamation declaring the month of October as Fire Prevention 
Month: Mary Springer and Bryan Russell addressed the Board regarding the proclamation declaring the month 
of October as Fire Prevention Month. Ms. Springer read the proclamation and asked that it be approved. Mr. 
Russell spoke about the history of fire prevention week. Supervisor Thompson thanked Ms. Springer for 
coming out to his district with this information. He further thanked Bryan Russell for the work that he does. 
Supervisor Williams made a motion  to approve a proclamation declaring the month of October as Fire 
Prevention Month; motion seconded by Supervisor Whitesinger. Vote unanimous approving the motion.  
c. Consideration and possible approval of FY2016 Homeland Security Grant Program Awards : Mary Springer 
discussed the FY2016 RAC Grant awards from Homeland Security. She stated that this year, there was over 
$400,000.00 in grant funds.  She spoke about training and exercise, which our portion of that is $21,798; the 
Citizen Corp grant in the amount of $9,434; and the Navajo County regional dispatch protocols in the amount 
of $43,000, with a total amount of $74,232. Supervisor Thompson made a motion to approve the FY2016 
Homeland Security Grant Program Awards ; motion seconded by Supervisor Williams . Vote unanimous 
approving the motion.  
COUNTY ATTORNEY:  Consideration and possible acceptance of Attorney General’s Victim’s R ights Grant 
Agreement #2017-010: Roxanne Padilla discussed the item and answered questions of the Board  indicating this 
is a reoccurring grant. Supervisor Williams made a motion to accept Attorney General’s Victim’s Rights Grant 
Agreement #2017 -010; motion seconded by Supervisor Black . Vote unanimous approving the motion.  
Supervisor Thompson spoke about the Domestic Violence Awareness kick-off event. 
NAVAJO COUNTY PUBLIC HEALTH SERVICES DISTRICT:  Board of Directors Session: Supervisor 
Williams made a motion  to enter into the Navajo County Public Health Services District Board of Directors 
Session; motion seconded by Supervisor Whitesinger. Vote unanimous approving the motion.  
a. Consideration and possible approval of Professional Services Contract with Jessica Vicari, RDH, for dental 
hygiene services through the Navajo Nation Oral Health Program effective October 1, 2016 to June 30, 2017 
in the amount of $40.00 per hour : Mary Herring discussed the professional services contract s with Jessica 
Vicari and Erica Forth Boswell. Supervisor Thompson made a motion to approve the professional contracts 
with Jessica Vicari, RDH, for dental hygiene services through the Navajo Nation Oral Heal th Program 
effective October 1, 2016 to June 30, 2017 in the amount of $40.00 per hour and the professional services 
contract with Erica Forth Boswell, RDH, for dental hygiene services through the Navajo Nation Oral Health 
Program effective October 1, 2016  to June 30, 2017 in the amount of $40.00 per hour ; motion seconded by 
Supervisor Williams. Vote unanimous approving the motion.

<!-- page 4 -->
b. Consideration and possible approval of Professional Services Contract with Erica Forth Boswell, RDH, for 
dental hygiene services through the Navajo Nation Oral Health Program effective October 1 2016 to June 30, 
2017 in the amount of $40.00 per hour: Mary Herring discussed the item.  
Supervisor Williams  made a motion  to return to regular session; motion seconded by Supervisor 
Whitesinger. Vote unanimous approving the motion.  
PUBLIC WORKS: Tool Box Presentation: Glenn Kephart gave a short presentation on Tools for Success. Mr. 
Kephart presented each Supervisor with a tool box. The Supervisors thanked Mr. Kephart for having the 
Employee Appreciation luncheon for Public Works and for all the work he does in all districts.  Supervisor 
Thompson spoke about respect between different cultures.  Supervisor Williams thanked Mr. Kephart for his 
leadership.  Supervisor Black expressed appreciation to Mr. Kephart.  Mr. Jayne indicated that Mr. Kephart 
made each tool box individually for all members of his team. Mr. Jayne thanked Mr. Kephart for his leadership.  
WHITE MOUNTAIN LAKES COUNTY RECREATION IMPROVEMENT DISTRICT: Boar d of 
Directors Session:  Supervisor Williams made a motion to enter into the White Mountain Lakes Board of 
Directors Session; motion seconded by Supervisor Thompson. Vote unanimous approving the motion. 
a. Consideration and possible acceptance of the resignat ion of Jerry McGraw from the White Mountain Lakes 
County Recreation Improvement District Advisory Committee and appoint Marc Silliman as Chairman and 
William Kaiser as Vice -Chairman:  Melissa Buckley discussed the item and requested that the Board of 
Directors ratify the decision of the Advisory Committee.  Supervisor Whitesinger made a motion to accept 
the resignation of Jerry McGraw  from the White Mountain Lakes County Recreation Improvement District 
Advisory Committee and appoint Marc Silliman as Chairman and William Kaiser as Vice-Chairman; motion 
seconded by Supervisor Thompson.  Vote unanimous approving the motion.  Ms. Buckley advised that the 
Advisory Committee will be soliciting applications for the vacant position. 
b. Consideration and possible approval of hiring an Interim Administrator for the White Mountain Lakes County 
Recreation Improvement District Advisory Committee for no more than 10 hours per week at $13.95 an hour:  
Ms. Buckley discussed the item and spoke about the person who was hire d, Pat Stevens, who worked the 
Labor Day weekend and did a very good job.   She spoke about the need for this position for the benefit of 
the community me mbers, to check the post office and  the local operations of the building and the district 
members.  Supervisor Williams made a motion to follow the recommendations of the Advisory Committee 
to hire an Interim Administrator for the White Mountain Lakes County Recreation Improvement District 
Advisory Committee for no more than 10 hours per week at $13.95 an hour; motion seconded by Supervisor 
Thompson.  Vote unanimous approving the motion. 
c. Consideration and possible approval to amend definitions A-5 and A-16 in Rule Book to have assignment of 
use privileges include renters of commercial property in the Distri ct and allow up to five people on a Joint 
Tenancy deed to be recognized as members:  Ms. Buckley discussed the item.  Supervisor Williams requested 
background on this item.  Ms. Buckley advised the Board that definition A-5 used to state that it is a perso n 
who pays rent to an owner  and who resides in the residence .  She stated that there is property that is leased , 
and the Advisory Committee wanted to make it more effective for people leasing or renting the property to 
have use passes, which would make it more accessible to people living in the district.  Supervisor Williams 
made a motion to approve the amended definitions A -5 and A-16 in Rule Book to have assignment of use 
privileges include renters of commercial property in the District and allow up to five people on a Joint Tenancy 
deed to be recognized as members ; motion seconded by Supervisor Black.  Vote unanimous approving the 
motion. 
 Supervisor Williams made a motion  to return to regular session; motion seconded by  Supervisor 
Whitesinger.  Vote unanimous approving the motion.

<!-- page 5 -->
COUNTY BUSINESS UPDATE:  Report from Board members, County Attorney and County Manager : 
Supervisor Whitesinger gave a report from District V. She expressed appreciation to the Audubon Society who 
invited her to their pu blic meeting.  She spoke about the importance of communication with developers and 
property owners and expressed gratitude to Mr. Kephart and his leadership in that respect.  She stated that this is 
her fourth year to do the welcome for the Tour of the Whi te Mountains, which welcomed over 400 bike riders.  
Supervisor Black gave a report from District I.  He expressed appreciation for the donation of the Chevrolet 
pickup truck to the Kayenta Volunteer Fire Department.   He stated that he  attended several counc il meetings. 
Supervisor Williams did not have a report from District IV. Vice-Chairman Thompson gave a report from 
District II.  He spoke about the funding for the improvements to the H60 roads.  He stated that he attended the 
NACOG Transportation Subcommi ttee.  He stated that he is working on having tax dollars returned from the 
State of Arizona to the Tribal Communities.  Chairman Whiting gave a report from District III.  He spoke about 
the Natural Resource Working Group  and their goals and priorities .  He spoke about the RealAZ Economic 
Development meeting.  He further spoke about the Domestic Violence Event in Holbrook.   He stated that his son 
got his first elk. 
ADJOURN: 10:53 a.m.  Supervisor Thompson made a motion to adjourn; motion seconded by  Supervisor 
Williams. Vote unanimous approving the motion.  
 
APPROVED:       DATED:  October 25, 2016 
 
Jason E. Whiting, Chairman 
Navajo County Board of Supervisors 
 
 
 
ATTEST: 
 
 
Melissa W. Buckley, Clerk 
Navajo County Board of Supervisors
